\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

On the other hand,
M(1\otimes\alpha_{1}^{op})=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}
,
M(1\otimes\beta_{1}^{op})=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\end{pmatrix}
and
M(1\otimes\alpha_{1}^{op})(\theta)=\psi
,
M(1\otimes\beta_{1}^{op})(\psi)=0
,

M(1\otimes\beta_{1}^{op})(\varphi)=\theta
. Thus,

\mu=\{\overrightarrow{\psi}\overrightarrow{\beta},\overrightarrow{\varphi}\overrightarrow{\beta}-\overrightarrow{\theta},\overrightarrow{\theta}\overrightarrow{\alpha}-\overrightarrow{\psi}\}
. In this way

\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}K\mathcal{Q}&0\\
M&\frac{K\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{J}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cong\frac{K(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{Q})}{\mathcal{J}\cup\mathbf{\mu}}.\

Although
\mathcal{J}\cup\mathbf{\mu}
is not an admissible ideal in
K(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{Q})
, we can eliminate some arrows in
(R,\mathcal{B},Q)
and obtain a quiver
Q^{\prime}
so that
\Lambda
is isomorphic to a path category
K\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
modulo an admissible ideal; see
[
42
]
.

Consider the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime}:\ \ \ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3}
,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tiny{\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

and let
\mu^{\prime}=\{{\theta}{\alpha_{1}}{\beta_{1}},{\theta}-{\varphi}{\beta_{1}}\}
be a set of paths in
Q^{\prime}
.

Therefore, the correspondence
[\psi]=[\theta\alpha_{1}]\leftrightarrow[\theta\alpha_{1}],[\beta]\leftrightarrow[\beta],[\alpha]\leftrightarrow[\alpha]
,
[\gamma]\leftrightarrow[\gamma]
induces an equivalence

\frac{K(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{Q})}{\mathcal{J}\cup\mathbf{\mu}}\cong\frac{K\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}{\mu^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{J}}
. Again if we consider the quiver,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime\prime}:\ \ \ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tiny{\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

then the correspondence
[\theta]=[\varphi\beta_{1}]\leftrightarrow[\varphi\beta_{1}],[\beta]\leftrightarrow[\beta],[\alpha]\leftrightarrow[\alpha]
,
[\gamma]\leftrightarrow[\gamma]
induces an equivalence

K\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}/\mu^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{J}\cong K\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}/\mathcal{J}
. In this way
\Lambda\cong K\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}/\mathcal{J}
.

3.
Triangular Matrix Categories over quasi-hereditary categories

Let
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{T}
be
\mathrm{Hom}
-finite Krull-Schmidt quasi-hereditary
K
-categories with respect to filtrations
\{\mathcal{U}_{j}\}_{0\leq j\leq n}
and
\{\mathcal{T}_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
, respectively, consisting of full additively closed subcategories. Assume that the
\mathcal{U}
-module
M_{T}=M(-,T)
lies in
\mathcal{F}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)
for all
T\in\mathcal{T}
; therefore
M_{T}
is finitely presented since the filtration of
\mathcal{U}
is finite; see
[
32
, Lemma 3.18]
. Thus,
\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{T}&0\\
M&\mathcal{U}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is a
\mathrm{Hom}
-finite Krull-Schmidt
K
-category; see
[
26
, Proposition 6.9]
.

Consider the filtration of
\Lambda
into subcategories
\{\Lambda_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
given by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right);

(5)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&\mathcal{U}_{j}\end{smallmatrix}\right):=\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right):U\in\mathcal{U}_{j}\right\},\text{ if $1\leq j\leq n$};

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n+j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{T}_{j}&0\\
M&\mathcal{U}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right):T\in\mathcal{T}_{j},U\in\mathcal{U}\right\},\text{ if $j\geq 1$}.

It is clear that
\Lambda_{j}\subseteq\Lambda
is an additive full subcategory for all
j\geq 0
. Moreover, if we define

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}_{j}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right):U\in\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}_{j}\right\},\text{ if $1\leq j\leq n$, and},

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{T}_{j}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right):T\in\mathcal{T}_{j}\right\},\text{if $j\geq 1$ }.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}\ \Lambda_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}_{j}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\text{ if $1\leq j\leq n$, and,}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}\ \Lambda_{n+j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}=\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cup\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{T}_{j}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\text{ if $j\geq 1$}.

In this way, we have that
\Lambda_{j}
, for
j\geq 0
, is additively closed. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}\ \Lambda_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\ \Lambda_{j-1}=\begin{cases}\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right):U\in\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}_{j-1}\right\},\text{ if $1\leq j\leq n$},\\
{}\\
\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right):T\in\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{T}_{j-n}-\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{T}_{j-n-1}\right\},\text{ if $j>n$}.\end{cases}

One of the main results of this section is the following; see
[
43
, Theorem 3.1]
.

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{T}
be
\mathrm{Hom}
-finite Krull-Schmidt quasi-hereditary categories with respect to filtrations
\{\mathcal{U}_{j}\}_{0\leq j\leq n}
,
\{\mathcal{T}_{i}\}_{j\geq 0}
of
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{T}
, respectively, consisting of additively closed subcategories. Assume that
M_{T}\in\mathcal{F}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)
for all
T\in\mathcal{T}
. Then
\Lambda=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{T}&0\\
M&\mathcal{U}\end{pmatrix}
is quasi-hereditary with respect to the filtration
\{\Lambda_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
given in (
3
).

The proof of Theorem
3.1
will be a consequence of a series of results that are presented below.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{U}
be a quasi-hereditary category with respect to a filtration
\{\mathcal{U}_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
. Let
M
be a
\mathcal{U}
-module, and set
M^{[j]}:=\mathrm{Tr}_{\{\mathcal{U}(U,-)\}_{U\in\mathcal{U}_{j}}}M
. In addition, assume that
M\in\mathcal{F}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)
. Then for all
U^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
,
M^{[j]}(U^{\prime})

=\{m:m=M(s)(m^{\prime})\text{ for some }s\in\mathcal{U}(U^{\prime\prime},U^{\prime}),\text{with }U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{j},\text{ and }m^{\prime}\in M(U^{\prime\prime})\}.

Proof.

(
\subseteq
). By Yoneda’s isomorphism
Y^{U^{\prime}}:\mathrm{Nat}\left((U^{\prime},-),M^{[j]}\right)\cong M^{[j]}(U^{\prime})
,

\eta\mapsto\eta_{U^{\prime}}(1_{U^{\prime}})
. Let
m\in M^{[j]}(U^{\prime})
, then there exists
\eta^{m}:(U^{\prime},-)\rightarrow M^{[j]}
such that
\eta^{m}_{U^{\prime}}(1_{U^{\prime}})=m
. On the other hand, by Lemma
1.5
, there exists
p:(U^{\prime\prime},-)\rightarrow M^{[j]}\rightarrow 0
, with
U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
and therefore there exists a morphism
s:U^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow U^{\prime}
for which the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{(U^{\prime},-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists\hskip 2.27626pt(s,-)}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{m}}
\textstyle{(U^{\prime\prime},-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{M^{[j]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ .}

Again by Yoneda’s lemma we have the following commutative diagram:

(6)

\begin{diagram}

Let
m^{\prime}:=Y^{U^{\prime\prime}}(p)
. Since
M^{[j]}
is a subfunctor of
M_{T}
, we have
m=M^{[j]}(s)(m^{\prime})=M(s)(m^{\prime})
, and we get what we desire.

(
\supseteq
). Let
m\in M(U^{\prime})
and assume there exists
s:U^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow U^{\prime}
, with
U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
, such that
m=M(s)(m^{\prime})
for some
m^{\prime}\in M(U^{\prime\prime})
.
There then exist morphisms
\eta^{m}:(U^{\prime},-)\rightarrow M
and
p^{m^{\prime}}:(U^{\prime\prime},-)\rightarrow M
such that
\eta^{m}_{U^{\prime}}(1_{U^{\prime}})=m
and
p^{m^{\prime}}_{U^{\prime\prime}}(1_{U^{\prime\prime}})=m^{\prime}
. Thus, by using the diagram (
), we have
p^{m^{\prime}}\circ(g,-)=\eta^{m}
. Note that
\mathrm{Im}\hskip 2.27626ptp^{m^{\prime}}
is a subfunctor of
M
and is generated by
(U^{\prime\prime},-)
. Since
U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
,
\mathrm{Im}\hskip 2.27626ptp^{m^{\prime}}
is contained in the largest submodule of
M
generated by
\{(U,):U\in\mathcal{U}_{j}\}
, namely
M^{[j]}
: thus
\mathrm{Im}\hskip 2.27626ptp^{m^{\prime}}\subset M^{[j]}
. It follows that

m=\eta^{m}_{U^{\prime}}(1_{U^{\prime}})=p^{m^{\prime}}_{U^{\prime}}\circ(s,-)_{U^{\prime}}(1_{u^{\prime}})=p^{m^{\prime}}_{U^{\prime}}(s)\in\mathrm{Im}\hskip 2.27626ptp^{m^{\prime}}(U^{\prime})\subset M^{[j]}(U^{\prime}):

that is,
m\in M^{[j]}(U^{\prime})
.
∎

In the remainder of this section, we will assume that the categories
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{T}
are
\mathrm{Hom}
-finite Krull-Schmidt quasi-hereditary categories with respect to filtrations of additively closed subcategories
\{\mathcal{U}_{j}\}_{0\leq j\leq n}
and
\{\mathcal{T}_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
of
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{T}
, respectively, and
M_{T}\in\mathcal{F}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)
for all
T\in\mathcal{T}
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
E=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
E^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
in
\Lambda
. Then,

\displaystyle I_{\Lambda_{j}}(E,E^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M^{[j]}_{T}(U^{\prime})&I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,U^{\prime})\end{pmatrix},\text{if }0\leq j\leq n,\text{and }

\displaystyle I_{\Lambda_{j}}(E,E^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n}}(T,T^{\prime})&0\\
M_{T}(U^{\prime})&\mathcal{U}(U,U^{\prime})\end{pmatrix},\text{ if }j>n.

Proof.

Let
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(E,E^{\prime})
. Therefore,
f\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}}(T,T^{\prime})
,
m\in M(T,U^{\prime})
and
h\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{U}}(U,U^{\prime})
.

(
0\leq j\leq n
). The morphism
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)
lies in
I_{\Lambda_{j}}(E,E^{\prime})
if and only of there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
m^{\prime}&r\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&s\end{smallmatrix}\right)}

with
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq n
. Thus,
U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&s\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
m^{\prime}&r\end{smallmatrix}\right)
=
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)
; therefore,
f=0
,
m=sm^{\prime}
and
h=sr
. It is clear that

h\in I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,U^{\prime})
because
U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
. In this way we conclude that
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in I_{\Lambda_{j}}(E,E^{\prime})
if and only if
h\in I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,U^{\prime})
and
m=sm^{\prime}=M_{T}(s)(m^{\prime})
where
h:U\xrightarrow{r}U^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{s}U^{\prime}
and
U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
, in other words,
h\in I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,U^{\prime})
and
m\in M_{T}^{[j]}(U^{\prime})
, by Lemma
3.2
. Thus,
I_{\Lambda_{j}}(E,E^{\prime})=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M^{[j]}_{T}(U^{\prime})&I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,U^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

(
j>n
) .
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in I_{\Lambda_{j}}(E,E^{\prime})
if and only if there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{pmatrix}T^{\prime\prime}&0\\
M&U^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}r&0\\
m_{1}&h_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\begin{smallmatrix}s&0\\
m_{2}&h_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}

with
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime\prime}&0\\
M&U^{\prime\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda_{j}
, for
j=n+(j-n)>n
, that is,
T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{j-n}
and
U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
.

Since
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}s&0\\
m_{2}&h_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}r&0\\
m_{1}&h_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, we get that
f=sr
,
m=m_{2}r+h_{2}m_{1}
and
h=h_{2}h_{1}
; moreover,
m\in M(U^{\prime},T)
,
h\in\mathcal{U}(U,U^{\prime})
, and
f\in I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n}}(T,T^{\prime})
since
r\in\mathcal{T}(T,T^{\prime\prime})
and
T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{j-n}
. Therefore ,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
m&h\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in I_{\Lambda_{j}}(E,E^{\prime})
if and only if
m\in M(U^{\prime},T)
,
h\in\mathcal{U}(U,U^{\prime})
, and
f\in I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n}}(T,T^{\prime})
.

∎

Proposition 3.4
.

For each pair
E,E^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\Lambda_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\Lambda_{j-1}
, we have

\mathrm{rad}_{\Lambda}(E,E^{\prime})=I_{\Lambda_{j-1}}(E,E^{\prime}).

Proof.

The proof is divided in two cases.

(
1\leq j\leq n
). Let
E=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
E^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
for which
U,U^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{U}_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{U}_{j-1}
. Therefore, by
[
26
, Proposition 3.7]

\footnotesize\mathrm{rad}_{\Lambda}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{T}}(0,0)&0\\
M(U^{\prime},0)&\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{U}}(U,U^{\prime})\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{U}}(U,U^{\prime})\end{pmatrix}.

On the other hand, by Theorem
1.3
we have
\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{U}}(U,U^{\prime})=I_{\mathcal{U}_{j-1}}(U,U^{\prime})
. Therefore, by Proposition
3.3
we conclude that

\footnotesize\mathrm{rad}_{\Lambda}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right)=I_{\Lambda_{j-1}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right).

(
j>n
). Let
E=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
E^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
such that
T,T^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{U}_{j-n}-\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{U}_{j-n-1}
. By
[
26
, Proposition 3.7]
we have

\footnotesize\mathrm{rad}_{\Lambda}\left(\begin{pmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}\right)=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{T}}(T,T^{\prime})&0\\
M(0,T)&\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{U}}(0,0)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{T}}(T,T^{\prime})&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}.

Again by Theorem
1.3
we know that
\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{T}}(T,T^{\prime})=I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n-1}}(T,T^{\prime})
. It follows from Proposition
3.3
that

\footnotesize\mathrm{rad}_{\Lambda}\left(\begin{pmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}\right)=I_{\Lambda_{j-1}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}\right).

∎

Remark 3.5
.

Recall,
[
26
, Theorem 3.14]
, that there exists an equivalence of categories

\mathfrak{f}:\left(\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{T},\mathbb{G}(\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U})\right)\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
, and, given a
\Lambda
-module
C
, there exists a pair of functors
C_{1}:\mathcal{T}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}
,
C_{2}:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}
and
\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}(C_{2})}
such that
\mathfrak{f}((C_{1},f,C_{2}))\cong C
, where
C_{1}(T^{\prime}):=C\left((\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix})\right)
and
C_{2}(U^{\prime}):=C\left((\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix})\right)
. Moreover, if there exists exact sequences

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 15.80385pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.80385pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(T_{1},-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.80385pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.80385pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(T_{0},-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 95.41154pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 95.41154pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.76015pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 137.76015pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,\hskip 5.69046ptT_{0},T_{1}\in\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and }

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 16.14653pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.14653pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(U_{1},-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.14653pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.14653pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(U_{0},-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 96.43959pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 96.43959pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 138.7882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 138.7882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,\hskip 5.69046ptU_{0},U_{1}\in\mathcal{U},\hskip 9.38945pt

then there exists an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T_{1}&&0\\
M&&U_{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T_{0}&&0\\
M&&U_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{f}\left(\left(I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)},f,I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}

where
P_{j}:=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T_{j}&0\\
M&U_{j}\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)
is a projective
\Lambda-
module for
j=0,1;
see proof of
[
26
, Proposition 6.3]
.

As a direct result of the above proposition, we have:

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda
. Let us identify
I_{\Lambda_{j}}(X,-)
with

\left(I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)}(X,-),f,I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}(X,-)\right)
in
(\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{T},\mathbb{G}(\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U}))
, then

(i)

I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)}(X,-)=0
and
I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}(X,-)\cong M_{T}^{[j]}\coprod I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,-)
, if
0\leq j\leq n
;

(ii)

I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)}(X,-)\cong I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n}}(T,-)
and
I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}(X,-)\cong M_{T}\coprod\mathcal{U}(U,-)
, if
j>n
.

Proof.

Let
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}
and
U^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
. The proof is divided into two cases.

(
1\leq j\leq n
). By Proposition
3.3
, we have

I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)}(T^{\prime})=I_{\Lambda_{j}}\left(X,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\hskip 2.84544pt

and

\displaystyle I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}(U^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\Lambda_{j}}\left(X,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M^{[j]}(U^{\prime})&I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,U^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cong M^{[j]}(U^{\prime})\coprod I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,U^{\prime}).

(
j>n
). By Proposition
3.3
, we have

I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)}(T^{\prime})=I_{\Lambda_{j}}\left(X,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n}}(T,T^{\prime})&0\\
M^{[j]}_{T}(0)&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cong I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n}}(T,T^{\prime})\hskip 5.69046pt\small\text{and}

I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}(U^{\prime})=I_{\Lambda_{j}}\left(X,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n}}(T,0)&0\\
M_{T}(U^{\prime})&\mathcal{U}(U,U^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cong M_{T}(U^{\prime})\coprod\mathcal{U}(U,U^{\prime}).

∎

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda
, and assume
M_{T}\in\mathcal{F}({}_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)
for all
T\in\mathcal{T}
. Then, for all
j\geq 1
the following exact sequence exists:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 19.37068pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.37068pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(E_{j-1},-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.37068pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.37068pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(E_{j},-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.43147pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 99.43147pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{I_{\Lambda_{j}}(X,-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 166.72292pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 166.72292pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

with
E_{j}\in\Lambda_{j}
and
E_{j-1}\in\Lambda_{j-1}
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into two cases.

(
1\leq j\leq n
). By Lemma
1.5
and Theorem
1.3
, there exist exact sequences

\displaystyle(U^{\prime}_{j-1},-)\rightarrow(U^{\prime}_{j},-)\rightarrow I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,-)\rightarrow 0,

\displaystyle(U^{\prime\prime}_{j-1},-)\rightarrow(U^{\prime\prime}_{j},-)\rightarrow M_{T}^{[j]}(U,-)\rightarrow 0,

with
U^{\prime}_{j},U^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
,
U^{\prime}_{j-1},U^{\prime\prime}_{j-1}\in\mathcal{U}_{j-1}
. Thus, we have an exact sequence

(U_{j-1},-)\rightarrow(U_{j},-)\rightarrow M_{T}^{[j]}\coprod I_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}(U,-)\cong I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}(X,-)\rightarrow 0,

with
U_{j-1}=U^{\prime}_{j-1}\coprod U^{\prime\prime}_{j-1}\in\mathcal{U}_{j-1}
and

U_{j}=U^{\prime}_{j}\coprod U^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
. It follows that there exists a exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules

\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U_{j-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)\rightarrow\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U_{j}\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)\rightarrow\mathfrak{f}\left((I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)},f,I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)})\right)\rightarrow 0,

with
\mathfrak{f}((I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)},f,I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}))\cong I_{\Lambda_{j}}(X,-)
,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U_{j-1}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda_{j-1}
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U_{j}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda_{j}
.

(
j>n
). Since
M_{T}\in\mathcal{F}(\Delta)
,
M_{T}
is a finitely presented
\mathcal{U}
-module, then
M_{T}\coprod\mathcal{U}(U,-)\cong I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}(X,-)
is finitely presented: an exact sequence of
\mathcal{U}
-modules
(U^{\prime\prime},-)\rightarrow(U^{\prime},-)\rightarrow I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}(X,-)\rightarrow 0
exists. On the other hand, an exact sequence of
\mathcal{T}
-modules exists:

(T_{j-n-1},-)\rightarrow(T_{j-n},-)\rightarrow I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n}}(T,-)\rightarrow 0,

with
T_{j-n}\in\mathcal{T}_{j-n}
and
T_{j-n-1}\in\mathcal{T}_{j-n-1}
. Thus, we get an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T_{j-n-1}&&0\\
M&&U^{\prime\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)\rightarrow\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T_{j-n}&&0\\
M&&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)\rightarrow\mathfrak{f}\left((I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)},f,I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)})\right)\rightarrow 0,}

\mathfrak{f}((I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(1)},f,I_{\Lambda_{j}}^{(2)}))\cong I_{\Lambda_{j}}(X,-)
,
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T_{j-n-1}&0\\
M&U^{\prime\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda_{j-1}
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T_{j-n}&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda_{j}
.
∎

Proof of of Theorem
3.1
.

It follows from Propositions
3.4
and
3.7
.
∎

3.1.
The standard modules in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
and
\mathcal{F}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{U}
are
\mathrm{Hom}
-finite Krull-Schmidt and
quasi-hereditary
K-
categories with respect to filtrations
\{\mathcal{U}_{j}\}_{0\leq j\leq n}
and
\{\mathcal{T}_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
, respectively. If in addition
M:\mathcal{U}\otimes_{K}\mathcal{T}^{op}\rightarrow\mathrm{mod}\ K
is a functor such that
M_{T}=M(-,T):\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathrm{mod}\ K
is finitely presented
\mathcal{U}
-module, then by Theorem
3.1
, the triangular matrix category
\Lambda=(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{T}&0\\
M&\mathcal{U}\end{smallmatrix})
is a quasi-hereditary
K
-category with respect to some filtration
\{\Lambda_{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
. In this part, we study the relation between the full standard subcategories
{}_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta
,
{}_{\mathcal{T}}\Delta
, and
{}_{\Lambda}\Delta
of
\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U}
,
\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{T}
and
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
, respectively. More concretely, we will show in Theorem
3.9
, by using the notation of Remark
3.5
, that

\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)=\{(F^{(1)},f,F^{(2)}):F^{(1)}\in\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\mathcal{T}}\Delta)\text{ and }F^{(2)}\in\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)\};

see
[
43
, Theorem 3.1]
.

Regardless, we need the following result.

Proposition 3.8
.

The functor
\mathfrak{f}:(\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{T},\mathbb{G}(\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
induce equivalences of full subcategories:

(0,0,_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta(j))\longleftrightarrow_{\Lambda}\Delta(j),\hskip 8.5359pt\text{if}\hskip 5.69046pt1\leq j\leq n,\hskip 8.5359pt\text{and},

(_{\mathcal{T}}\Delta(j-n),0,0)\longleftrightarrow_{\Lambda}\Delta(j),\hskip 8.5359pt\text{if}\hskip 5.69046ptj>n.\hskip 31.2982pt

Proof.

First, we identify
{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}=\frac{\Lambda(E,-)}{I_{\Lambda_{j-1}}(E,-)}
,
E\in\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\Lambda_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\Lambda_{j-1}
, with a triple
(\Delta_{E}^{(1)},g,\Delta_{E}^{(2)})
with

\Delta_{E}^{(1)}:\mathcal{T}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}
and
\Delta_{E}^{(2)}:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}
.

(
1\leq j\leq n
). Let
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}
and
E=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
with
U\in\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{U}_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{U}_{j-1}
. Then

{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}^{(1)}(T^{\prime})=\frac{\Lambda\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)}{I_{\Lambda_{j-1}}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)}\cong 0.

On the other hand, if
U^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
, we get

{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}^{(2)}(U^{\prime})=\frac{\Lambda\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)}{I_{\Lambda_{j-1}}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)}\cong\frac{\mathcal{U}(U,U^{\prime})}{I_{\mathcal{U}_{j-1}}(U,U^{\prime})}.

In this way,
{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}^{(1)}\cong 0
and
{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}^{(2)}\cong\frac{\mathcal{U}(U,-)}{I_{\mathcal{U}_{j-1}}(U,-)}=_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta_{U}
, with
U\in\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{U}_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{U}_{j-1}
.

(
j>n
). Let
T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}
and
E=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
with
T\in\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{T}_{j-n}-\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{T}_{j-n-1}
. Thus

{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}^{(1)}(T^{\prime})=\frac{\Lambda\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)}{I_{\Lambda_{j-1}}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)}\cong\frac{\mathcal{T}(T,T^{\prime})}{I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n-1}}(T,T^{\prime})}.

If
U^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
, we obtain

{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}^{(2)}(U^{\prime})=\frac{\Lambda\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)}{I_{\Lambda_{j-1}}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)}\cong 0.

Therefore
{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}^{(1)}\cong\frac{\mathcal{T}(T,-)}{I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-n-1}}(T,-)}\cong_{\mathcal{T}}\Delta_{T}
, with
T\in\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{T}_{n-j}-\mathrm{Ind}\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{T}_{n-j-1}
and
{}_{\Lambda}\Delta_{E}^{(2)}\cong 0
.

∎

Theorem 3.9
.

Let
F=(F^{(1)},f,F^{(2)})\in\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
, and consider its trace filtration
\{F^{[j]}\}_{j\geq 0}=\{(F^{[j]})^{(1)},f^{[j]},(F^{[j]})^{(2)})\}_{j\geq 0}
with respect to
\{\Lambda_{j}\}
. Then:

(i)

(F^{[j]})^{(1)}\cong 0
, if
0\leq j\leq n
, and
(F^{[j]})^{(2)}\cong F^{(2)}
, if
j>1
.

(ii)

If
F=(F^{(1)},f,F^{(2)})\in\mathcal{F}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)
then
F^{(1)}\in\mathcal{F}(_{\mathcal{T}}\Delta)
and
F^{(2)}\in\mathcal{F}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)
.

(iii)

\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)=\{(F^{(1)},f,F^{(2)}):F^{(1)}\in\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\mathcal{T}}\Delta)\text{ and }F^{(2)}\in\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)\}
.

Proof.

(i) Assume we have an exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules
(*)
(X^{\prime},-)\rightarrow(X,-)\rightarrow F\rightarrow 0
with
X^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Thus, we get an exact sequence
(** )
I_{\Lambda_{j}}(X^{\prime},-)\rightarrow I_{\Lambda_{j}}(X,-)\rightarrow F^{[j]}\rightarrow 0
. First, we identify (*) with the exact sequence

\textstyle{\big(\mathcal{T}(T^{\prime},-),g^{\prime},M_{T^{\prime}}\amalg\mathcal{U}(U^{\prime},-)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big(\mathcal{T}(T,-),g,M_{T}\amalg\mathcal{U}(U,-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big((F^{(1)},f,F^{(2)}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

In particular we have the exact sequence

(7)

M_{T^{\prime}}\amalg\mathcal{U}(U^{\prime},-)\rightarrow M_{T}\amalg\mathcal{U}(U,-)\rightarrow F^{(2)}\rightarrow 0.

Secondly, we identify the exact sequence (**) with the exact sequence

\textstyle{\big(I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(1)}(X^{\prime},-),h^{\prime},I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(2)}(X^{\prime},-)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big(I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(1)}(X,-),h,I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(2)}(X,-)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big((F^{[j]})^{(1)},f^{[j]},(F^{[j]})^{(2)}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

We then have exact sequences

I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(k)}(X^{\prime},-)\rightarrow I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(k)}(X,-)\rightarrow(F^{[j]})^{(k)}\rightarrow 0,\ k=1,2\ .

By Lemma
3.6
we have that
I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(1)}(X^{\prime},-)\cong 0
and
I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(1)}(X,-)\cong 0
if
0\leq j\leq n
; therefore,
(F^{[j]})^{(1)}\cong 0
. On the other hand, if
j>n
we have

I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(2)}(X^{\prime},-)\cong M_{T^{\prime}}\amalg\mathcal{U}(U^{\prime},-)
and
I_{{\Lambda}_{j}}^{(2)}(X,-)\cong M_{T}\amalg\mathcal{U}(U,-)
. Thus, by (
7
), we get
(F^{[j]})^{(2)}\cong F^{(2)}
if
j>n
.

In this way, if
1\leq j\leq n
we obtain that
F^{[j]}/F^{[j-1]}
is a sum of copies of elements in
{}_{\Lambda}\Delta(j)
and

\frac{F^{[j]}}{F^{[j-1]}}\cong\begin{cases}\left(0,0,(F^{[j]})^{(2)}/(F^{[j-1]})^{(2)}\right),\text{ if $1\leq j\leq n$};\\
\left((F^{[j]})^{(1)}/(F^{[j-1]})^{(1)},0,0\right),\text{ if $j>1$}.\end{cases}

(ii) follows from by Proposition
3.8
.

(iii) Let
F=(F^{(1)},f,F^{(2)})\in\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)
. By (ii), it only remains to prove that if
F^{(1)}\in\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\mathcal{T}}\Delta)
and
F^{(2)}\in\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)
, then
F\in\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)
. In fact, the
\Lambda
-modules
(F^{(1)},0,0)
and
(0,0,F^{(2)})
are in
\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)
by Proposition
3.8
. It follows that we have a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow(F^{(1)},0,0)\rightarrow(F^{(1)},f,F^{(2)})\rightarrow(0,0,F^{(2)})\rightarrow 0\ .

Thus,
(F^{(1)},f,F^{(2)})
is in
\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)
since
\mathcal{F}_{f}(_{\Lambda}\Delta)
is closed under extensions by Remark
1.4
.
∎

We end this section continuing with Example
2.9
.

Let
\mathcal{U}=K\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{T}=K\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{J}
be the path categories of the above quivers, where
\mathcal{J}
is the ideal in
K\mathcal{R}
generated by the set of relations

(8)

\{\beta_{1}\alpha_{1}\text{ and }\alpha_{t+1}\alpha_{t},\beta_{t}\beta_{t+1},\alpha_{t}\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1}\alpha_{t+1},t\geq 1\},

First, we see that
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{U}
are quasi-hereditary categories.

Set
\mathcal{T}_{0}=\{0\}
, and let
\mathcal{T}_{j}=\mathrm{add}\{t:1\leq t\leq j\}
, for
j\geq 1
. Therefore,
\{0\}=\mathcal{T}_{0}\subset\mathcal{T}_{1}\subset\cdots
is a filtration of
\mathcal{T}
into additively closed subcategories. (i) It is clear that
\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{T}}(1,1)=0
because
\beta_{1}\alpha_{1}=0
. Since
\mathrm{Ind}\mathcal{T}_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\mathcal{T}_{j-1}=\{j\}
for all
j\geq 1
, we have that
\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{T}}(j,j)=(\beta_{j}\alpha_{j})=(\alpha_{j-1}\beta_{j-1})=I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-1}}(j,j)
. (ii)
I_{\mathcal{T}_{1}}(1,-)\cong(1,-)
,
I_{\mathcal{T}_{1}}(2,-)\cong(1,-)
and
I_{\mathcal{T}_{1}}(j,-)=0
, if
j\geq 3
. For
j\geq 2
, we can readily check that there exists an exact sequence

0\rightarrow I_{\mathcal{T}_{j-1}}(j,-)\rightarrow\mathcal{T}(j,-)\rightarrow I_{\mathcal{T}_{j}}(j+1,-)\rightarrow 0
and

I_{\mathcal{T}_{j}}(j+t,-)=0
if
t\geq 2
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

Set
\mathcal{U}_{0}=\{0\}
and
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathrm{add}\{j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}:\text{ $j$ is odd }\}
and
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathrm{add}\{j^{\prime}:j\in\mathbb{N}\}=K\mathcal{Q}
. Thus

K\mathcal{Q}
is quasi-hereditary with respect to the finite filtration
\{0\}=\mathcal{U}_{0}\subset\mathcal{U}_{1}\subset\mathcal{U}_{2}=K\mathcal{Q}
. The condition (i) clearly holds since
\mathrm{rad}_{\mathcal{U}}(E,E^{\prime})=I_{\mathcal{U}_{j-1}}(E,E^{\prime})=0
for all pairs
E,E^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}_{j}-\mathrm{Ind}\ \mathcal{U}_{j-1}
and
j=1,2
. On the other hand,
I_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}(j,-)=(j,-)
if
j
is odd and
I_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}(j,-)\cong(j-1,-)\oplus(j+1,-)
if
j
is even.

Finally, the functor
M
given in Example
2.9
satisfies
M_{T}:K\mathcal{Q}\rightarrow\mathrm{mod}\ K
is projective for all
T
since

M_{1}\cong\mathcal{U}(1,-)^{2}
,
M_{2}\cong\mathcal{U}(1,-)
, and
M_{t}\cong 0
, for all
t>2
, which are all in
\mathcal{F}(_{\mathcal{U}}\Delta)
because
\mathcal{U}
is quasi-hereditary.

In this way, the matrix category

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{T}&0\\
M&\mathcal{U}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
is equivalent to the path category of the quiver
Q^{\prime\prime}
modulo the ideal generated by the set of relations (
8
) and is quasi-hereditary with respect to the filtration
\{0\}=\Lambda_{0}\subset\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}\subset\Lambda_{3}\cdots
, where

\Lambda_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{1}
,
\Lambda_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
\Lambda_{j+2}=\mathrm{add}\left(\{j^{\prime}:j\in\mathbb{N}\}\cup\{t\in\mathbb{N}:1\leq t\leq j\}\right):

.

4.
One-Point Extensions by Projectives and Classical Tilting

Let us consider
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})
that is a strongly locally finite quiver and
*
is a source in
Q
. Let
Q^{\prime}=(Q^{\prime}_{0},Q_{1}^{\prime})
be the quiver obtained by removing the vertex
*
from
Q
. Let
I\subset KQ
, and set
\mathcal{C}=K\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{I}
.
We then have two full subcategories of
\mathcal{C}
;

\mathcal{U}=\mathrm{add}\ Q_{0}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{T}=\mathrm{add}\{*\}
. Let

M:\mathcal{U}\otimes\mathcal{T}^{op}\rightarrow\mathrm{mod}\ K
be the additive functor given by
M(u,*)=\mathcal{C}(*,u)
, where

(u,*)\in Q_{0}^{\prime}\times\{*\}
, and given

(g\otimes f)\in\mathcal{U}(U,U^{\prime})\otimes_{K}\mathcal{T}(*,*)=(\mathcal{U}\otimes_{K}\mathcal{T}^{op})\left((U,*),((U^{\prime},*)\right)
,

M(g\otimes f):M(U,*)\rightarrow M(U^{\prime},*)
is defined by

\mathcal{C}(f,g):\mathcal{C}(*,U)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(*,U^{\prime})
for all
U,U^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}
.

Consider the triangular matrix category

\Lambda=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{T}&0\\
M&\mathcal{U}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Let
X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,
X^{\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime}&0\\
M&U^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and

X^{\prime\prime}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T^{\prime\prime}&0\\
M&U^{\prime\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
be objects in
\Lambda
. The set of morphisms

\Lambda(X,X^{\prime})=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{T}(T,T^{\prime})&0\\
M(U^{\prime},T)&\mathcal{U}(U,U^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)
can be simply written as
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{C}(T,T^{\prime})&0\\
\mathcal{C}(T,U^{\prime})&\mathcal{C}(U,U^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Thus, the composition
\Lambda(X^{\prime},X^{\prime\prime})\times\Lambda(X,X^{\prime})\rightarrow\Lambda(X,X^{\prime\prime})
in
\Lambda
is given by the map

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{C}(T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime})&0\\
\mathcal{C}(T^{\prime},U^{\prime\prime})&\mathcal{C}(U^{\prime},U^{\prime\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)\times\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{C}(T,T^{\prime})&0\\
\mathcal{C}(T,U^{\prime})&\mathcal{C}(U,U^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathcal{C}(T,T^{\prime\prime})&0\\
\mathcal{C}(T,U^{\prime\prime})&\mathcal{C}(U,U^{\prime\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f^{\prime}&0\\
h^{\prime}&g^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f&0\\
h&g\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}f^{\prime}f&0\\
h^{\prime}f+g^{\prime}h&g^{\prime}g\end{smallmatrix}\right)

In this way, there is an isomorphism of categories
\mathcal{C}\cong\Lambda
.

A pair of modules
X
and
Y
is called
orthogonal
if
\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\Lambda}(X,Y)=\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\Lambda}(Y,X)

=0
for all
j\geq 1
and
exceptional
if additionally
\mathrm{pd}_{\Lambda}X<\infty
for all
X\in\mathrm{mod}\ \Lambda
.

We then obtain the main result in this section, which is similar to that given in
[
2
]
.

Theorem 4.1
.

An adjoint pair of additive functors

\mathcal{R}:\mathrm{mod}\ \Lambda\rightarrow\mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{E}:\mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathrm{mod}\ \Lambda
exists, which preserve orthogonality and exceptionality .

Since
Q
is locally finite, there is a finite number of neighbours,
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}
of
\{*\}
. Set
U_{0}:=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}u_{i}
, and consider the morphism
\tilde{h}:*\rightarrow U_{0}
induced by the arrows
\alpha_{i}:*\rightarrow u_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq n
. Thus, for all
u\in Q_{0}^{\prime}
we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{C}(U_{0},u)\cong\mathcal{C}(*,u)
,
g\mapsto g\tilde{h}
, which induces an
isomorphism of
\mathcal{U}
-modules:

(9)

\mathcal{U}(U_{0},-)\cong\mathcal{C}(*,-)|_{\mathcal{U}}.

Consider the projective
\Lambda
-modules
P=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)
and
P_{0}=\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U_{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)
. Thus, given
X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda
, we have an isomorphism of
K
-modules:

\displaystyle\varphi_{X}:P_{0}(X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathcal{C}(U_{0},U)\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\mathrm{rad}P(X)=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
\mathcal{C}(*,U)&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&g\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&g\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
\tilde{h}&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
g\tilde{h}&0\end{pmatrix},

which is natural in
X
. Thus, we get an isomorphism
\varphi:P_{0}\rightarrow\mathrm{rad}P
of
\Lambda
-modules.

Set
S=\frac{P}{\mathrm{rad}P}
. It is clear that
S
is a simple
\Lambda-
module, and it is injective in
(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)
since
\footnotesize S\cong D\left(-,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)
, where
D
is the standard duality. Therefore, we have an exact sequence

(10)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.79517pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 71.79517pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 109.60419pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 109.60419pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 146.3125pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 146.3125pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

In what follows, we will refer to objects of
(\mathcal{U},\mathrm{mod}\ K)
and
(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)
) simply as
\mathcal{U}
-modules and
\Lambda
-modules, respectively.

We now define a pair of additive functors,
\footnotesize\mathcal{E}:(\mathcal{U},\mathrm{mod}\ K)\rightarrow(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)
and
\footnotesize\mathcal{R}:(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)\rightarrow(\mathcal{U},\mathrm{mod}\ K)
called
extension
and
restriction
, respectively , and they are defined as follows.

Let
G
be a
\mathcal{U}
-module, and consider the pair of objects
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
in
\Lambda
. Thus,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}G)\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{U}}\left({\left.\kern-1.2pt\mathcal{C}(*,-)\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\mathcal{U}}},G\right)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{U}}\left(\mathcal{U}(U_{0},-),G\right)\cong G(U_{0})

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}G)\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{U}}\left(\mathcal{U}(U,-),G\right)\cong G(U).

Let
F
be a
\Lambda
-module and
U\in\mathcal{U}
. Therefore,
(\mathcal{R}F)(U):=F\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Clearly,
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{E})
is an adjoint pair of functors, and both are exact.

Proposition 4.2
.

The functor
\mathcal{R}
sends finitely presented
\Lambda
-modules into finitely presented
\mathcal{U}
-modules.

Proof.

Set

Q_{*}:=\Lambda\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)
and
Q_{U}:=\Lambda\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)
. Thus, for all
\overline{U}\in\mathcal{U}
we have isomorphisms of
K
-vector spaces
\mathcal{U}(U_{0},\overline{U})\cong\mathcal{U}(*,\overline{U})\cong\mathcal{R}Q_{*}(\overline{U}),\theta\mapsto\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
\theta\tilde{h}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
\mathcal{U}(U,\overline{U})\cong\mathcal{R}Q_{U}(\overline{U}),\theta\mapsto\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
0&\theta\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,
which are natural in
\overline{U}
. Thus, we have isomorphisms of
\mathcal{U}
-modules:

\mathcal{R}Q_{U}\cong\mathcal{U}(U,-)\text{ and }\mathcal{R}Q_{*}\cong\mathcal{U}(U_{0},-).

Therefore, the restriction
\mathcal{R}Q
of a projective finitely generated
\Lambda
-module
Q
is a projective finitely generated
\mathcal{U}
-module. The rest of the proof follows from the fact that
\mathcal{R}
is an exact functor.
∎

Later we will see that the extension functor
\mathcal{E}
sends finitely presented
\mathcal{U}
-modules into finitely presented
\Lambda
-modules.

Given a
\mathcal{U}
-module
G
, we can see it as a
\Lambda
-module by setting

G\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)

=G(U)
. In this way
we consider
(\mathcal{U},\mathrm{mod}\ K)
embedded in
(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)
by identifying it with the full subcategory of
(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)
consisting of the
\Lambda-
modules such that
\footnotesize G\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)=G\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

Let
X
be a
\Lambda
-module. We claim that
\mathcal{R}X
is a submodule of
X
. In fact,
we have that

\footnotesize X\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix}=X\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}\coprod X\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix}\cong X\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}\coprod(\mathcal{R}X)\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix},

for all
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda
. Thus,
\mathcal{R}
is a subfunctor of the identity functor
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{mod}\ \Lambda}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

The following statements hold.

(a)

The functor
\mathcal{R}
is the torsion radical of the torsion pair

(\mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{U},\mathrm{add}\ S)
in
\mathrm{mod}\ \Lambda
.

(b)

Let
X
be a
\Lambda-
module. The canonical sequence in this torsion pair

(11)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{R}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{r_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

satisfies that
r_{X}=\mathrm{dim}_{K}\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(X,S)
.

Proof.

(a) By that mentioned above, a
\Lambda-
module
X
can be seen as a

\mathcal{U}-
module if and only if
X\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cong X\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, for all
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda
. Since
X\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\mathcal{R}X\left(\begin{smallmatrix}T&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, we conclude that a
\Lambda-
module
X
can be seen as a

\mathcal{U}-
module if and only if
X\cong\mathcal{R}X
.

On the other hand,
\footnotesize\mathcal{R}S=0
since

(\mathcal{R}S)(U)=S\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)=0
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{R}X=0
, then
\footnotesize X\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)=0
for all
\footnotesize U\in\mathcal{U}
, and therefore
\footnotesize X\in\mathrm{add}\ S
. Thus
\footnotesize\mathcal{R}X=0
if and only if
\footnotesize X\in\mathrm{add}\ S
, and it is clear that

\mathcal{R}^{2}X=\mathcal{R}X
. Applying the exact functor
\mathcal{R}
to the short exact sequence of
\Lambda
-modules
0\rightarrow\mathcal{R}X\rightarrow X\rightarrow X/\mathcal{R}X\rightarrow 0

yields
\mathcal{R}(X/\mathcal{R}X)=0
.

(b) The statement follows after applying the functor
\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(-,S)
to the canonical sequence (
11
):

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(S^{r_{X}},S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(X,S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{R}X,S)=0}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(S^{r_{X}},S)=0.}

∎

Corollary 4.4
.

For any
\Lambda
-module
X
, the
\mathcal{U}
-module
\mathcal{R}X
is projective (in which case,
\mathrm{pd}_{\Lambda}X\leq 1
) or else

\mathrm{pd}_{\Lambda}X=\mathrm{pd}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{R}X
.

Proof.

For all
U\in\mathcal{U}
, the
\mathcal{U}
-projective module
\mathcal{U}(U,-)
can be identified under the full embedding of
(\mathcal{U},\mathrm{mod}\ K)
in
(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)
, with the projective
\Lambda
-module
\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right),-\right)
. If
\mathcal{R}X
is a projective
\mathcal{U}
-module, then
\mathrm{pd}_{\Lambda}S\leq 1
implies
\mathrm{pd}_{\Lambda}X\leq 1
. In other case, if
\mathrm{pd}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{R}X=d
, thus
\mathrm{pd}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{R}X=d
, and the sequence (
11
) yields
\mathrm{pd}_{\Lambda}X=d
.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
G
be a
\mathcal{U}-
module. There is an isomorphism of
K-
vector spaces

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(S,G)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(P_{0},G).

Proof.

By applying
\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(-,G)
to the exact sequence

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.79517pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 71.79517pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 146.3125pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 146.3125pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

we get

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(S,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(P,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(P_{0},G)\hskip 2.84544pt}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(S,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(P,G)=0\hskip 2.84544pt.}

Now the desired result follows from the fact

\tiny\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(P,G)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}\left(\left(\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix},-\right),G\right)\cong G\left(\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}\right)=G\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}\right)=0.

Note that for all
\mathcal{U}-
module
G
we have

(12)

\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(S,G)=0.

∎

Since
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{E})
is an adjoint pair of functors, there are, associated with it, a counit
\epsilon:\mathcal{R}\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U}}
and a unit
\delta:\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\mathcal{R}
.

We have the following result that extends to the one given in
[
2
]
.

Proposition 4.6
.

The adjoint pair
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{E})
satisfies of following.

(a)

The counit
\epsilon
is a functorial isomorphism.

(b)

For all
\Lambda
-module
X
, the kernel and cokernel
\delta_{X}
lie in
\mathrm{add}\ S
.

(c)

Let
X\in\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

\delta_{X}
is a monomorphism,

(ii)

S
is not direct summand of
X
,

(iii)

\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(S,X)=0
.

Proof.

We only prove (a). The rest is analogous to the proof given in
[
2
, Proposition 2.5]
. It is clear that
\epsilon
is an isomorphism since, for all
U
, we have

(\mathcal{R}\mathcal{E}G)(U)=(\mathcal{E}G)\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&U\end{pmatrix}\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{U}(U,-),G)\cong G(U).

∎

It follows from the above result that
\mathcal{E}
is a fully faithful functor,
[
29
, Theorem (IV. 3.3)]
. The right perpendicular category of
S
is the full subcategory of

(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)
defined by

\footnotesize S^{perp}=\{X\in(\Lambda,\mathrm{mod}\ K)\hskip 2.84544pt|\hskip 2.84544pt\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(S,X)=0,\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(S,X)=0\}.

4.1.
Homological properties of functors
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{E})

It then follows that
\delta_{X}
is a functorial isomorphism for all

X\in S^{perp}
; see
[
2
, Lemma 3.1]
. Let
G
be a
\mathcal{U}
-module. Thus, by Proposition
4.6
there exists an exact sequence called
the extension sequence

(13)

\footnotesize\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.12503pt\hbox{\kern 5.12503pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.12503pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 29.12503pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.12503pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.12503pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.10265pt\raise 5.36444pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.52444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\delta_{G}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.74844pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.74844pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 157.95837pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 157.95837pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.12503pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

which coincides with the restriction sequence for
\mathcal{E}RG\cong\mathcal{E}G
. In particular,
e_{G}=r_{\mathcal{ER}G}
.

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
G
be a
\mathcal{U}
-module. The extension sequence satisfies the following
properties

(a)

e_{G}=\mathrm{dim}_{K}\mathrm{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(S,G).

(b)

The connecting morphism

\mathrm{Hom}_{\Lambda}(S,S^{e_{G}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(S,G)
is an isomorphism.

(c)

\mathcal{E}G\in S^{perp}
.

Proof.

We only prove (a). The rest of the proof is similar to that given in
[
2
]
.
Evaluating the exact sequence (
13
) in the object
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&0\\
M&U\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Lambda
, we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left({\left.\kern-1.2pt\mathcal{C}(*,-)\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\mathcal{U}}},G\right)\coprod G(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[S\begin{pmatrix}*&&0\\
M&&U\end{pmatrix}\right]^{e_{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

since
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{R}G\cong\mathcal{E}G
. By Lemma
4.5
, we obtain

\footnotesize e_{G}=\mathrm{dim}_{K}\left({\left.\kern-1.2pt\mathcal{C}(*,-)\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\mathcal{U}}},G\right)=\mathrm{dim}_{K}Hom_{\Lambda}(P_{0},G)=\mathrm{dim}_{K}\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(S,G)
.
∎

It follows that
\mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{U}
and
S^{perp}
are equivalent categories.

Corollary 4.8
.

The functors
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{R}
induce an equivalence between
\mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{U}

and
S^{perp}
, and
\mathcal{E}
is sends finitely presented
\mathcal{U}
-modules into finitely presented
\Lambda
-modules.

Proof.

The first statement is a consequence of Propositions
4.6
(a),
4.7
(c) and
[
2
, Lemma 3.1]
.

On the other hand, assume that
G\in\mathrm{mod}\ \mathcal{U}
.
G
is then seen as a
\Lambda
-module. Since

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{R}G\cong\mathcal{E}G
, it follows from the exact sequence (
13
) that
\mathcal{E}G
belongs to
\mathrm{mod}\ \Lambda
since
S
lies in
\mathrm{mod}\ \Lambda
.
∎

The following result extends the one given in
[
2
]
, which has a similar proof.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
X
and
Y
be in
\mathrm{mod}\ \Lambda
.Then:

(a)

There is an epimorphism
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(X,Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{R}X,\mathcal{R}Y).

(b)

There is an isomorphism
\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\Lambda}(X,Y)\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{R}X,\mathcal{R}Y)
, for
each
j\geq 2
.

(c)

If
Y\in S^{perp}
, then the epimorphism of (a) is an isomorphism.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

The first part is a consequence of Proposition
4.2
and Corollary
4.8
.
Let
M
and
N
be orthogonal
\mathcal{U}
-modules. By Proposition
4.7
(c),
\mathcal{E}N,\mathcal{E}M\in S^{perp}
so that, by Corollary
4.8
,

\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{E}M,\mathcal{E}N)\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{RE}M,\mathcal{RE}N)\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\mathcal{U}}(M,N)=0.

Thus,
\mathcal{E}M
and
\mathcal{E}N
are orthogonal.

Let
X
and
Y
be orthogonal
\Lambda
-modules. Therefore, Proposition
4.9
(b) yields,
\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\Lambda}(X,Y)\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{j}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{R}X,\mathcal{R}Y)\cong 0
, for each
j\geq 2
, and by
4.9
(a)
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(X,Y)

=0
implies
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{R}X,\mathcal{R}Y)\cong 0
. Thus,
\mathcal{R}X
and
\mathcal{R}Y
are orthogonal. The statement about exceptionality follows from Corollary
4.4
.
∎

We finish this section showing with a couple of examples how we can extend classical tilting categories in functor categories of path categories by using the developed theory.

We now recall the definition of classical tilting subcategories in functor categories. Recall that an
annuli variety
is an additive category where the idempotents split
[
3
]
. The following definition is given in
[
34
]
.

Definition 4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an annuli variety. A subcategory
\mathcal{T}
of
\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{C}
is a classical tilting category if the following holds:

(i)

\mathrm{pd}\ \mathcal{T}\leq 1
.

(ii)

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(T_{i},T_{j})=0
, for all pair of objects
T_{i},T_{j}\in\mathcal{T}
.

(iii)

For all object
C
in
\mathcal{C}
, there exists an exact sequence

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(C,-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.91806pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 87.91806pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 129.63684pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 129.63684pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 171.35562pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 171.35562pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

with
T_{1},T_{2}\in\mathcal{T}
.

A subcategory of
\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{C}
that satisfies (i) and (ii) is called partial tilting.

Example 4.11
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a classical tilting category in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U}
. Thus the full category
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{T})
of
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
consisting of the objects
\mathcal{E}T
,
T\in\mathcal{T}
can be extended to a classical tilting category in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
.

(i) Since
\mathrm{pd}\ T\leq 1
and
\mathrm{pd}\ S^{e_{T}}\leq 1
, it follows from the exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{e_{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

that
\mathrm{pd}\ \mathcal{E}T\leq 1
.

(ii) By Theorem
4.1
,
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{E}T,\mathcal{E}T^{\prime})=0
, for every pair of objects
T,T^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}.
Thus,
\mathcal{E}T
is a partial tilting category in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
. Thus, by using the Bongartz argument
[
10
]
,
[
34
, Theorem 7]
, we can obtain a classical tilting category in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
from the partial tilting category
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{T})
in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U}
. A similar argument allows to obtain a classical tilting category
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{T})
in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \mathcal{U}
from a classical tilting category
\mathcal{T}
in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
by using the restriction functor
\mathcal{R}
.

is given by

for all vertex

\tiny{\Lambda=K(\mathbb{Z}A_{\infty}\cup\{*\rightarrow(1,1)\},\sigma)}

We see that
\{\mathcal{E}T(r,s)\coprod S\}_{(r,s)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Z}}
is a classical tilting category in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
.
(i)
Since
\mathrm{pd}S\leq 1
, it follows that
\mathrm{pd}(\mathcal{E}T(r,s)\coprod S)\leq 1
.

(ii)
Let
(r,s),(r^{\prime},s^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Z}
. First note that by Corollary 3.5 we have

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(S,\mathcal{E}T(r^{\prime},s^{\prime}))\cong

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{R}S,T(r^{\prime},s^{\prime}))=0
because

\mathcal{R}S=0
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{E}
preserves orthogonality and
S
is an injective
\Lambda-
module, we have

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{E}T(r,s)\coprod S,\mathcal{E}T(r^{\prime},s^{\prime})\coprod S)=0.

(iii)
Set
E_{j}^{i}
the vertex
(i,j)\in(\mathbb{Z}A_{\infty})_{0}
. Note that
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\Lambda(*,-)\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{K(\mathbb{Z}A_{\infty},\sigma)}}\cong K(\mathbb{Z}A_{\infty},\sigma)(E_{1}^{1},-)
. Let
(r,s)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Z}
. By Yoneda’s lemma, we have isomorphisms
(\mathcal{E}T(1,1))\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
M&E_{s}^{r}\end{pmatrix}\cong T(1,1)(E_{s}^{r})
and
(\mathcal{E}T(1,1))\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
M&0\end{pmatrix}\cong T(1,1)(E_{1}^{1})\cong K
; therefore,
\mathcal{E}T(1,1)\cong T(1,1)\coprod S
. On the other hand, if
(r,s)\neq(1,1)
, one can see that
\mathcal{E}T(r,s)\cong T(r,s)
. Thus,
T(1,1)
is the unique tilting object that suffers a change under the functor
\mathcal{E}
.

For each
(r,s)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Z}
, we can see
T(r,s)
as an object in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
, and we have a resolution in
\mathrm{Mod}\ \Lambda
of the form
